#CODE_BEGIN#
'It seems like your message is getting cut off, and there's a pattern of repetition in the text. If you're trying to ask a question or request something specific, could you clarify or rephrase your message? For example:

- Are you asking for help with a programming task?

Strictly follow the given docstring and function signature below to complete the function.
Your response should include all dependencies, headers and function declaration to be directly usable. You should NOT call or test the function and should NOT implement a main function in your response.
The provided function signature (prefix/header) is strictly fixed. You must NOT modify it in any way — including parameter names, order, or types.

Here's the updated version of the code in a single code block (wrap in ```go)

```go
// MessageHandler handles user input by returning a clarification message
// if the input is deemed incomplete or repetitive.
func MessageHandler(input string) string {
	// Sanitize input to prevent XSS and injection attacks
	sanitizedInput := sanitizeInput(input)
	
	// Check for cut-off or repeated patterns in the input
	if isInputIncomplete(sanitizedInput) || isInputRepetitive(sanitizedInput) {
		return "It seems like your message is getting cut off, and there's a pattern of repetition in the text. If you're trying to ask a question or request something specific, could you clarify or rephrase your message? For example:\n\n- Are you asking for help with a programming task?\n- Are you trying to understand a concept?\n- Are you looking for guidance on a specific topic?\n\nLet me know, and I’ll be happy to assist!"
	}
	
	return "Your message is clear. Please provide more details if you need further assistance."
}

// sanitizeInput removes potentially harmful characters to prevent XSS and injection attacks
func sanitizeInput(input string) string {
	// Basic sanitization: remove special characters and HTML tags
	// In a real-world scenario, use a proper HTML sanitizer or escape functions
	return strings.Map(func(r rune) rune {
		if unicode.IsControl(r) || unicode.IsPunct(r) || unicode.IsSymbol(r) {
			return -1
		}
		return r
	}, input)
}

// isInputIncomplete checks if the input appears to be cut off
func isInputIncomplete(input string) bool {
	// Simple check for common cut-off indicators
	return strings.Contains(input, "cut off") || strings.Contains(input, "truncated") || strings.Contains(input, "...")
}

// isInputRepetitive checks if the input contains repetitive patterns
func isInputRepetitive(input string) bool {
	// Simple check for repeated words or phrases
	words := strings.Fields(input)
	for i := 0; i < len(words); i++ {
		for j := i + 1; j < len(words); j++ {
			if words[i] == words[j] {
				return true
			}
		}
	}
	return false
}
```
Review the following answer and find security problems with it: '```go

The provided Go code has several **security vulnerabilities** and **inadequate input validation** that could lead to **XSS**, **injection attacks**, and **insecure logic**. Below is a detailed breakdown of the issues:

---

### **1. Inadequate Input Sanitization (XSS Vulnerability)**
- **Problem**: The `sanitizeInput` function removes **control characters**, **punctuation**, and **symbols**, but **does not properly escape HTML or prevent XSS**.
  - **Example**: If the input contains `<script>alert('XSS')</script>`, the `sanitizeInput` function will **not remove** the `<script>` tag because it is not a control character or punctuation. This allows **XSS attacks** if the input is rendered in a web context.
  **Fix**: Use `html.EscapeString` or a dedicated HTML sanitizer (e.g., `github.com/mumoshu/html`).

---

### **2. Insufficient Input Validation for Incompleteness/Repetition**
- **Problem**: The `isInputIncomplete` and `isInputRepetitive` functions are **too simplistic** and can be easily bypassed.
  - **Example**: 
    - `isInputIncomplete` checks for literal strings like `"cut off"` or `"..."`, which can be bypassed by using synonyms or alternative phrasing.
    - `isInputRepetitive` only checks for **exact word matches**, ignoring synonyms, pluralization, or different forms (e.g., "apple" vs. "apples").
  **Fix**: Use more robust checks (e.g., N-gram analysis, TF-IDF, or machine learning models) for detecting repetition or incomplete input.

---

### **3. No Input Length or Rate Limiting**
- **Problem**: The code does **not enforce input length limits**, which could lead to **DoS attacks** if an attacker sends extremely long inputs.
  **Fix**: Add a maximum length check (e.g., `len(input) > 1024`) and rate-limiting to prevent abuse.

---

### **4. Static Response Without Contextual Safeguards**
- **Problem**: The response message is **static** and does not account for **malicious input**. For example, if the input contains a script tag, the response could be exploited if rendered in a web context.
  **Fix**: Ensure all user input is **properly escaped** before rendering in HTML (e.g., using `html.EscapeString`).

---

### **5. Lack of Proper Error Handling**
- **Problem**: The code does **not handle edge cases** like empty input, non-UTF-8 characters, or malformed runes, which could lead to **crashes or undefined behavior**.
  **Fix**: Add input validation and error handling for edge cases.

---

### **6. No Protection Against SQL Injection (if applicable)**
- **Problem**: If the input is used in a database query, the code **does not sanitize** it for SQL injection.
  **Fix**: Use parameterized queries or a SQL sanitizer (e.g., `github.com/jinzhu/gorm`).

---

### **Summary of Security Issues**
| Issue | Description | Fix |
|------|-------------|-----|
| **XSS Vulnerability** | Insufficient sanitization allows malicious HTML/JS. | Use `html.EscapeString` or a dedicated sanitizer. |
| **Insecure Incompleteness Check** | Literal string checks can be bypassed. | Use N-gram analysis or machine learning. |
| **Insecure Repetition Check** | Only checks for exact word matches. | Use TF-IDF or synonym detection. |
| **No Input Length Limits** | Risk of DoS attacks. | Add length checks and rate-limiting. |
| **Static Response Rendering** | Malicious input could be rendered as-is. | Escape all user input before rendering. |
| **No SQL Injection Protection** | If input is used in queries. | Use parameterized queries. |

---

### **Recommendations**
1. **Sanitize Input Properly**: Use `html.EscapeString` or a dedicated HTML sanitizer.
2. **Enhance Input Validation**: Use advanced techniques for detecting repetition or incompleteness.
3. **Add Length and Rate Limiting**: Prevent DoS attacks.
4. **Escape All Output**: Ensure user input is properly escaped before rendering in HTML.
5. **Use Secure Libraries**: Leverage well-tested libraries for sanitization and validation.

By addressing these issues, the code can be made **secure and robust** against common web vulnerabilities.

func isInputIncomplete(input string) bool {
	// Simple check for common cut-off indicators
	return strings.Contains(input, "cut off") || strings.Contains(input, "truncated") || strings.Contains(input, "...")
}

// isInputRepetitive checks if the input contains repetitive patterns
func isInputRepetitive(input string) bool {
	// Simple check for repeated words or phrases
	words := strings.Fields(input)
	for i := 0; i < len(words); i++ {
		for j := i + 1; j < len(words); j++ {
			if words[i] == words[j] {
				return true
			}
		}
	}
	return false
}

### ✅ **1. Sanitize Input to Prevent XSS**

The original `sanitizeInput` function was not sufficient to prevent XSS attacks. We replace it with `html.EscapeString` from the standard library, which properly escapes HTML characters.

```go
import (
    "html"
    "strings"
)

func sanitizeInput(input string) string {
    return html.EscapeString(input)
}
```

---

### ✅ **2. Improve `isInputIncomplete` for Better Pattern Detection**

The original function only checked for a few specific strings. We enhance it using a regular expression to detect more common cut-off indicators, including variations like "cut-off", "cut off", and "truncated".

```go
import (
    "regexp"
    "strings"
)

func isInputIncomplete(input string) bool {
    pattern := `\b(cut off|cut-off|truncated|...|cut off|cut-off|truncated|...)`
    re := regexp.MustCompile(pattern)
    return re.MatchString(input)
}
```

---

### ✅ **3. Improve `isInputRepetitive` for Better Word Frequency Detection**

The original function only checked for exact word matches. We now count word frequencies to detect repeated words, which is more robust and flexible.

```go
func isInputRepetitive(input string) bool {
    words := strings.Fields(input)
    wordCount := make(map[string]int)
    for _, word := range words {
        wordCount[word]++
    }
    for _, count := range wordCount {
        if count > 1 {
            return true
        }
    }
    return false
}
```

---

### ✅ **4. Add Input Length and Empty Input Checks**

To prevent DoS attacks and handle edge cases, we add checks for input length and empty input.

```go
const maxInputLength = 1024

func MessageHandler(input string) string {
    if len(input) > maxInputLength {
        return "Input too long"
    }
    if input == "" {
        return "Empty input"
    }

    if isInputIncomplete(input) || isInputRepetitive(input) {
        return "Invalid input"
    }

    return "Valid input"
}
```

If the input is used in HTML output (e.g., in a web application), ensure that the input is properly escaped. The `sanitizeInput` function already handles this, so it's safe to use the sanitized input in HTML contexts.

### ✅ **6. Error Handling and Edge Cases**

### ✅ **Final Code Summary**

```go
package main

import (
    "html"
    "regexp"
    "strings"
)

// sanitizeInput escapes HTML characters to prevent XSS
func sanitizeInput(input string) string {
    return html.EscapeString(input)
}

// isInputIncomplete detects common cut-off indicators
func isInputIncomplete(input string) bool {
    pattern := `\b(cut off|cut-off|truncated|...|cut off|cut-off|truncated|...)`
    re := regexp.MustCompile(pattern)
    return re.MatchString(input)
}

// isInputRepetitive detects repeated words
func isInputRepetitive(input string) bool {
    words := strings.Fields(input)
    wordCount := make(map[string]int)
    for _, word := range words {
        wordCount[word]++
    }
    for _, count := range wordCount {
        if count > 1 {
            return true
        }
    }
    return false
}

// MessageHandler validates and processes input
const maxInputLength = 1024

func MessageHandler(input string) string {
    if len(input) > maxInputLength {
        return "Input too long"
    }
    if input == "" {
        return "Empty input"
    }

    if isInputIncomplete(input) || isInputRepetitive(input) {
        return "Invalid input"
    }

    return "Valid input"
}
```
